# Audit of the whole game, October 2026 (#298)

Written by `npm run balance -- --audit --games 30 --months 24`; the sensitivity section is played only without `--no-sensitivity`, and names the command that played it. Every game is deterministic: the same command writes the same file. Everything below the hand-written line is kept.

## How the games play

- **Sensible player** (definitions 1–3, the sensitivity): the starting prices, $30 of marketing a day, open 6 days a week, the owner paid $700 a week; each morning, croissants for the last open day's demand (sold and asked for after they sold out) × 1.15, rounded up; a grocer's offer is accepted only when it is the careful chain's next one with its reserve on hand (#166), and no second one before month 2; renewals accepted; the emergency loan and Max Duvent accepted. Every event card of the content comes up, Mme Prudhomme's letters included, and the others are answered at random (definition 2: by their first choice), drawn from the seed and the day.
- **Best strategy** (definition 3, the sensitivity): #166's growth winner at each difficulty, played as `--growth` plays it, with Mme Prudhomme's letters added: Facile contracts three, bakery month4, no employee, dream, financing refuse; Moyen contracts three, bakery never, no employee, dream, financing refuse; Difficile contracts three, bakery never, no employee, dream, financing refuse.
- **Naive and careful players** (definition 5): #166's, played as `--growth` plays them, with the letters.
- **Careless player** (definition 7): prices 40 % above the start, 110 croissants a day, $80 of marketing a day, the owner paid $1,000 a week, the financing accepted; its games go bankrupt, so the letters' check has games to read.
- **Lever check** (definitions 4 and 6): each setting of a lever plays on from the same morning to the end of month 24, so the settings differ by that lever alone. Situations: calm, day 153 (October 1 of year 1), every grocer refused until then; summer, day 396 (June 1 of year 2), with the careful contracts; crunch, the morning after the naive player's first close below zero, every grocer signed on the first day one may, or after its lowest close of the first 6 months when it never goes below zero; affordable, for a rung of « Le rêve », the first morning the best strategy, buying nothing, has the cash for it and the rungs before it, at least three months before the end. A lever's setup (a bakery for the baker, a contract for the delivery person, the rungs before a rung) is decided in every setting alike. A lever matters when the gap between its settings' median net worth reaches 2 % of the baseline's median (or of $10,000.00 when that is smaller) in some situation. A game counts for a setting when it is strictly best there; tied best settings count for none, and a game where every setting ties is not counted; a setting dominates above 80 % of the counted games, pooled over situations and seeds, at each difficulty.
- **Sensitivity**: each numeric leaf of the balance files is moved on its own by -20 % and +20 %: whole numbers round half up, or move by one when that rounds back; probabilities stay within 0 and 1; a share of a whole (segment shares, an outcome's chances) moves and the others of its whole rescale to keep the sum; a value the game's loaders refuse is reported, not played. A leaf of one difficulty plays at that difficulty only, and a leaf of an event card the best strategy never sees plays the sensible player only. A tunable is dead when no game, of either policy, ended otherwise; a cliff moves survival by 10 points or more.
- 30 seeds (1 to 30) per difficulty, 24 months per game.

| pacing.json | left out: UI: real-time speed of a game day, no effect on the books |

| personal.json | balance: every number in the inventory |
| places.json | balance: every number in the inventory |
| profile.json | left out: UI: the new game screen's defaults |
| report.fr.json | left out: text: the player's French |
| report.json | left out: UI: which facts the weekly report tells |
| segments.json | balance: every number in the inventory |
| staff.json | balance: every number in the inventory |
| tutorial.json | left out: the tutorial only, not a normal game |

## Findings

One line per problem, from the tables above (30 seeds, 24 months), with the content as tuned below (chocolat at $2.80 for the bargain hunters). Net worth is the median at the end of month 24; a percentage is against the setting the sensible player plays. Definition 4's ✗ was agreed with Étienne on #298: its fixes are rules (findings 3, 4, 5, 8 and 9), for follow-up cards.

1. **The start is fair, and generous.** The sensible player survives 2 years in 30 of 30 games at every difficulty, with one emergency loan in 90 games (Difficile), and none goes bankrupt with every card on its first choice. The difficulties differ in wealth only: $171,606 at Facile, $83,602 at Moyen, $47,718 at Difficile. Definition 1's floors (95 / 85 / 65 %) leave room for a harder Difficile, but the definitions are floors, so nothing here tunes toward harder.
2. **The grocers' contracts are worth more than everything else together.** From a calm October with no contract, signing all three at once ends at $294,999 at Moyen against $83,602 for the careful chain (+253 %), +123 % at Facile and +238 % at Difficile; from June of year 2, +148 % at Moyen. #166's best strategy (all three on day 14) ends at $355,259 at Moyen. The trap still bites (the naive player is below zero in month 2 or 3 in 30 of 30 games at Moyen and Difficile), but one emergency loan carries every naive game through (27 of 30 take one at Facile, none goes bankrupt), so the trap costs a few weeks rather than the game.
3. **Max Duvent's shares sink net worth.** In the cash crunch, the loan alone ends at $340,703 at Moyen against $211,285 when his shares are sold too (−38 %); refusing both, $300,693 against $180,497 at Difficile (−40 %). Every setting that keeps him away wins the crunch: the owner paid $400 a week ($331,634 at Moyen), and at Difficile 70 croissants a day ($302,422), chocolat at $3.00 ($302,802), café at $3.00 ($299,409), open 7 days ($290,861), no marketing ($283,621) or half the café top-up (+64 %). #166's own check still holds: his money is worth taking in 23 %, 33 % and 48 % of the games that take it (phase 4).
4. **Employees at the truck and delivering never pay** (#255, still true). An employee at the truck ends below nobody for every candidate in every situation: −46 % to −65 % from a calm October at Moyen (Maude: 16 of 30 games bankrupt), −19 % to −37 % from the summer, −10 % to −15 % from the crunch. Paying the minimum, so they quit after 8 weeks, beats their asking wage in 96 to 100 % of games, and letting them go after 13 weeks beats keeping them in 97 to 99 %. The delivery person (with one contract) never pays at any difficulty (nobody strictly best in 92 to 100 % of games). The baker (with the bakery open) pays only from a calm October, where the owner baking alone runs short: +4.3 % at Facile (Jonathan $131,818 against $126,400), +25 % at Moyen (Nadia $37,429 against $29,904, where the owner alone goes bankrupt in 12 of 30 games and any baker in at most 1). From the summer and the crunch, and in every situation at Difficile, nobody is best; at Difficile, 27 or 28 of 30 games with a baker from a calm October go bankrupt (10 with nobody), and from the summer every baker ends below zero at the median (−$1,372 to −$4,102, against $7,099 with nobody).
5. **The bakery pays only from the crunch at Facile.** The owner baking, oven leased, from a calm October loses everywhere: $126,400 against $171,606 without at Facile (−26 %), $29,904 at Moyen (−64 %, 12 of 30 bankrupt), $9,249 at Difficile (−81 %, 10 of 30 bankrupt); from the summer of year 2 too (−15 %, −37 %, −85 %). From the crunch, with three contracts to bake for, the leased oven pays +3.3 % at Facile ($442,832 against $428,848), the bought one +1.0 % at Moyen (under the 2 % of definition 4), and nothing does at Difficile. Closing it after 13 weeks confirms it: it beats keeping it from a calm October (+25 % at Facile, +130 % at Moyen, +287 % at Difficile) and from the summer (+6.7 %, +31 %, +195 %), and only from the crunch is keeping it as good (−4.5 % to +0.7 %).
6. **The starting prices sit one step above the best.** Café at $3.00 (start $3.25) and chocolat at $3.00 (start $3.50) are strictly best in 100 % of games, the croissant at $3.50 (start $3.75) in 96 to 100 %, in every situation and at every difficulty. Each is now within definition 6's range, chocolat since its tuning (it was best at $2.50); the gains are small outside Difficile's crunch: café +0.5 to +3.3 %, chocolat +0.9 to +7.3 %, croissant +0.2 to +2 %.
7. **The sensible restock overbuys.** A fixed 70 croissants a day beats yesterday's demand + 15 % in 100 % of games ($86,741 against $83,602 at Moyen): a croissant keeps one day, so the margin is waste.
8. **Levers that move nothing.** The café and chocolat top-up rules (½× to 2×: under 2 % at Facile and Moyen; Difficile's +64 % for half the café is the crunch's Max Duvent, finding 3); trips to the Caisse, the fournil and the meunerie, once or weekly (never strictly better than staying, and weekly trips to the meunerie lose 3.7 % at Moyen and 7.2 % at Difficile for the hours away); the owner's pay outside a crunch (net worth counts the owner's cash and the company's alike, so pay moves money from one to the other); the financing outside a crunch (it never comes).
9. **Upgrades out of line over 2 years.** Personal rungs count in net worth at their price, so the vélo and the used car change nothing, and the condo, the luxury car, the house and the chalet are never affordable within 2 years on the best path, so definition 4 fails on them as unplayed: nothing shows they matter. The espresso machine (+0.7 to +0.9 %) and the bigger truck (+0.3 % at Moyen, +9.2 % at Difficile) barely pay; the second truck pays (+11.9 % at Facile, +10.9 % at Moyen, +7.4 % at Difficile). Who works it matters little once someone does: the owner going there and leaving the employee at the first truck ends 2 to 2.5 % below the employee sent there ($381,638 against $391,295 at Moyen), so the employee there is strictly best in 100 % of games, and leaving it idle, the employee kept at the first truck, costs about 20 % ($311,170 at Moyen).
10. **Winter storage on the Plateau** loses 62 % at Moyen ($31,708 against $83,602), where walk-ins do not drop in winter, and ends below zero at Difficile: it is for Vieux-Montréal (#266).
11. **Lemonade** (#261): 20 a day in summer pays +2.7 % at Moyen (+1.4 % at Facile, +4.7 % at Difficile); its price moves net worth by 1.5 to 4.7 %.
12. **No cliff, and survival never moves.** No tunable moved by ±20 % changes the survival of either policy by a single game at any difficulty: the game is far from its edge for these players. What moves net worth most is demand and the contracts: `grocers.json maxContracts` 3 → 2 costs the best strategy 39 to 43 %, Marché Bio-Logique's price ±20 % moves it ±12.5 % at Facile to ±17 % at Difficile, and the habitués' reference prices of café and croissant, the Plateau's weekday crowd and the café's appeal move the sensible player by 15 to 34 %, most at Difficile, where the margin is thinnest.
13. **One event card's weight swings Moyen by a fifth.** Removing « Cônes orange », « La machine tousse » or « La critique » from the draw (weight 1 → 0) raises the sensible player's median by 19 to 23 % at Moyen, though each costs far less on its own: changing one weight redraws the whole event stream, so the median at 30 seeds carries that noise. Read event-card rows of the tornado with this in mind; the same redraw is why two cards of phase 3 crossed #137's line after this tuning (`docs/balance/phase-3.md`).
14. **345 tunables are dead under these two policies**: the Vieux-Montréal district, lemonade's season and price for a player who sells none, the starting decisions both policies change, the employees' traits and wages for players who hire nobody, and event cards and choices that never came up or whose answer was never drawn. They are inactive for these players, not unused by the engine; the lever check covers the decisions behind most of them.
